So `builder.Configuration.AddCommandLine(args)` produced NO key for `--Port:22222`. Why? In .NET 10 minimal hosting, command-line args that are NOT known host settings might be... wait the default CreateBuilder args feed already... Let me think about the ASP.NET Core host `--Port` handling. In ASP.NET Core minimal hosting, `WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args)` passes args to the host builder AND ALSO the command line config provider IS added by default. The default `AddCommandLine(args)` in CreateBuilder uses switch mappings, and unknown switches like `--Port` map to key "Port". Yet here nothing appears.

Key insight: In .NET 9+, the default command line config in CreateBuilder uses **no switch mapping for arbitrary keys**; it uses `CommandLineConfigurationProvider` with default mapping which DOES accept `--key=value` and `--key value` and `--key:value`? Actually `Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration.CommandLine` default: `--key=value`, `--key value`, `/key value`, `--key:value`? For the "--key:value" format: the default `CommandLineConfigurationProvider` splits on '=' OR treats "--key value" as (key, value). The "key:value" colon form may NOT be parsed by default — that's a `WebHost` filter for known switch mappings.

Hmm — but evidence: BEFORE-CLI shows NO Port key from CLI at all (only REPRO_PORT/PORT from env). And after AddCommandLine STILL no Port key. So `--Port:22222` produced nothing in both default and my explicit AddCommandLine. That means the colon form isn't parsed by CommandLineConfigurationProvider. The supported forms are `--key=value` and `--key value`.

So for CLI args to reach options, the arg format must be `--Port=17902` (equals form). The README may document the equals form. Let me verify: use `--Port=22222`.

Actually let me confirm quickly.

Also handle `--Port 17902` (space form) — the provider handles that natively.

199:     @staticmethod
200:     def _best_shape_item(doc):
201:         # Part.insert can yield objects whose Shape is a Shell (not a Solid) —
202:         # pick the first object that exposes the most usable solids, else the
203:         # last inserted object.
204:         best = None
205:         best_solids = -1

208:             if shp is None:

210:             solids = len(getattr(shp, "Solids", []))
211:             if solids > best_solids:

# Task 01 — API body binding contradicts the documented protocol

## Status
OPEN

## Context
`SubmitJobRequest(string? StlPath, string? OutputDir)` binds with ASP.NET's
default `JsonSerializerOptions.Web` (`camelCase`). The GENESIS spec documents
and curls the snake_case body:

```bash
-d '{"stl_path":"/data/part.stl","output_dir":""}'
```

Verified live: a snake_case body returns `400 missing_stl_path` while the
camelCase form succeeds. This means a GENESIS-compliant client cannot submit.

## Scope
- `src/fcadserve/Api/ApiModels.cs` (`SubmitJobRequest`)
- `src/fcadserve/Api/Endpoints.cs` (POST `/v1/jobs`)

## Requirements
Accept BOTH key spellings (snake_case and camelCase) for `stl_path`/`output_dir`
so the spec example works verbatim, without breaking existing camelCase clients.

Options: a tolerant `JsonConverter`/`[JsonPropertyName]`-style mapping, or a
normalized request DTO taking both spellings and resolving to a canonical one.

## Acceptance criteria
- `curl -X POST .../v1/jobs -d '{"stl_path":"/abs/path.stl","output_dir":""}'`
  returns 202 and queues a job (no `missing_stl_path`).
- camelCase body still works.
- Ambiguous bodies (both spellings set, different values) are rejected with an
  explicit error code rather than silently picking one.===== tasks/task04.md =====
# Task 04 — Command-line options can never override environment

## Status
OPEN

## Context
`Program.cs` builds configuration as:

```csharp
Configuration.AddJsonFile("fcadserve.json", optional: true)
    .AddEnvironmentVariables("FCADSERVE_")
    .AddCommandLine(args);
```

...then iterates `AsEnumerable()` (which respects precedence: CLI > env > json),
normalizes env keys, and re-adds them with `AddInMemoryCollection(normalized)`.
The in-memory provider is appended LAST, so it has the HIGHEST precedence —
the normalized copies of `FCADSERVE_*` values now shadow any `--Key=value`
command-line argument. CLI overrides are silently dead.

## Scope
- `src/fcadserve/Program.cs` (config assembly, `NormalizeConfigKey`)

## Requirements
Preserve intended precedence: `fcadserve.json` < env < command line.
Build the normalized collection from the ORIGINAL env provider only (not the
merged `AsEnumerable()`), or add the normalized provider BEFORE `AddCommandLine`,
or otherwise ensure CLI wins.

## Acceptance criteria
- Starting the service with `--Jobs__TimeoutSeconds=5` overrides
  `FCADSERVE_JOBS__TIMEOUT_SECONDS`.
- Existing env-only workflows (`launch_server.sh`) behave identically.
- Config from `fcadserve.json` still works and is the lowest precedence.===== tasks/task05.md =====
# Task 05 — Subprocess isolation is not what the config implies

## Status
OPEN

## Context
Two gaps:

1. `FreeCadCommandBuilder.ApplyUserConfigIsolation` sets `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` /
   `XDG_CACHE_HOME` / `XDG_DATA_HOME` per-job. Under the DEFAULT `flatpak`
   mode the sandbox largely ignores/owns those variables, so "isolate user
   config" is cosmetic in the mode it ships with. In `exec` mode the env vars
   DO reach the raw process (isolation works) but there is NO sandbox around it.

2. `Mode="exec"` runs the configured FreeCAD command with no containment:
   no bubblewrap, no resource limits, no RAM cap. Combined with
   `MaxInputSizeBytes = 512 MB` (Options/ServiceOptions.cs:91), a single job
   can balloon the host through a 600s FreeCAD session.

GENESIS requires hardening to be documented where it interacts with Flatpak
(GENESIS.md:209-210).

## Scope
- `src/fcadserve/FreeCad/FreeCadCommandBuilder.cs`
- `src/fcadserve/Options/ServiceOptions.cs` (`MaxInputSizeBytes` default,
  isolation options, optional bwrap wrapper)

## Requirements
Either make the isolation genuinely effective, or document accurately. Concretely:

- Lower the default `MaxInputSizeBytes` to a sane FreeCAD mesh limit
  (e.g. 50 MB) and document the knob; keep the option overridable.
- For `exec` mode: either wrap the command in a light sandbox
  (bwrap with read-only root policy + writable state dirs), or add explicit docs
  that `exec` implies trusting the host and the configured binary.
- Add a readiness check / doc note clarifying effective isolation per mode.

## Acceptance criteria
- A 512 MB STL is rejected by default; the configured lower limit is enforced
  both at submission and in the readiness docs.
- Documentation states exactly what isolation `flatpak` vs `exec` provides and
  how `XDG_*` is handled in each.
- Behavior unchanged for the flatpak default beyond the size-limit default.===== tasks/task06.md =====

# Task 08 — Minor correctness & hygiene cleanups

## Status
OPEN

## Context
Assorted low-priority findings from the review. Each is self-contained.

1. **`status_url` basePath inconsistency**
   `ApiModels.SubmitResponse` (Api/ApiModels.cs:11) emits `/v1/jobs/{id}`
   without the `Request.PathBase` prefix, while `JobToDto` (Api/ApiModels.cs:45)
   honors it. Behind a reverse proxy the queued response points at the wrong
   base. Make both consistent.

2. **`ServiceOptions.SectionName` unused / misleading**
   `SectionName` (Options/ServiceOptions.cs:10) is `"fcadserve"` but config
   binds at the ROOT (`Get<ServiceOptions>()`, Program.cs:46). A
   `fcadserve.json` file that nests a `"fcadserve": {...}` section silently
   won't bind. Either bind to the section or drop the constant / document that
   keys are root-level.

3. **`RenderOptions.Iso`/`Section` booleans are dead config**
   Jobs/JobExecutor.cs:30 always constructs `new RenderOptions(true, true, ...)`
   and the renderer `freecad/fcadserve_render.py` ignores them (it always
   attempts the section). Remove the booleans, keep width/height, and have the
   renderer bail non-fatally if iso/section were requested off.

4. **Unreachable code in worker**
   `freecad/fcadserve_worker.py` lines ~113-114, ~140-141, ~150-151:
   `self.fail_node(...)` always raises, so the subsequent
   `return self.finalize_failed()` is dead. Remove the dead returns or convert
   `fail_node` to return a sentinel and let callers unify the finalize path.

5. **Retention query shape**
   `PruneTerminalAsync` (Jobs/JobDatabase.cs:250-288) selects ALL terminal rows
   then takes `Skip(maxRetained)`. Fine at the 500 default, but the
   `LIMIT $keep` with `long.MaxValue` is misleading; either `ORDER BY`
   + real `LIMIT maxRetained OFFSET` offset computation or keep it simple with a
   documented assumption. Low value; do only if touching the file anyway.

6. **DB: no WAL / busy timeout**
   Single-process semaphore serialization is fine now, but adding
   `JournalMode = Wal` + `DefaultTimeout` to the connection string builder
   (Jobs/JobDatabase.cs:67-73) future-proofs concurrent readers and avoids
   `database is locked` if a second process ever reads the DB.

7. **`launch_server.sh` destructiveness**
   It `rm -rf`s `work/e2e_state`/`work/e2e_out` on every start. Add a warning
   and/or make the paths overridable so a dev never nukes an unrelated dir.

## Requirements
Pick up the items that make sense now (1, 2, 3 are quick and touch API surface);
4 & 7 are mechanical; 5 & 6 optional. Do NOT touch behavior covered by
task01-07 here (e.g. API spelling is handled in task01).

## Acceptance criteria
- Consistent `status_url` across responses; config-key documentation matches
  actual binding.
- No dead render config; worker has no unreachable fatal branches.
- Guard-rail in launch script (or documented override).
```

**Tool: edit**

**Input:**
```json
{

18: failure is never retried. The C# side then fails the job with `render_failed`
19: when a re-run could have succeeded.

22: - `freecad/fcadserve_render.py` (main, guard at top)
23: 
24: ## Requirements
25: Only treat the run as already-complete when the RESULT IS SUCCESSFUL:
26: require `render.result.json["ok"] == true` (and iso present). A failed first
27: attempt must be re-run on double execution.
28: 
29: ## Acceptance criteria
30: - Double execution after a successful render still short-circuits (exit 0).
31: - Double execution after a `ok:false` result re-runs the render instead of
32:   skipping, so transient/partial failures recover.

(End of file - total 32 lines)

From ApiModels.cs / Endpoints.cs:
- POST /v1/jobs — body accepts both snake_case (stl_path/output_dir) and camelCase (stlPath/outputDir). Ambiguous duplicates rejected. Returns 202 with {job_id, status: "queued", status_url}.
- Errors: 400 {code, error} with codes: missing_stl_path, relative_path, unsupported_extension, missing_file, input_is_directory, input_outside_allowed_root, input_too_large, output_is_file, relative_path, output_outside_allowed_root, artifact_collides_with_input, invalid_output_dir, invalid_json. 409 for output_already_exists / artifact_collides_with_input. 429 queue_full.
- GET /v1/jobs/{id} — returns job object: job_id, status, phase, created_at, started_at, finished_at, updated_at, stl_path, output_dir, effective_output_dir, input_size_bytes, input_sha256, exit_code, error_code, error, report_path, artifacts[], status_url.
- status values: queued, running, succeeded, skipped, failed, cancelled.
- phase values: queued, loading_stl, reconstructing, exporting_step, validating_step, rendering, finished (need to check exact enum), or whatever JobPhase has.
- GET /v1/jobs/{id}/artifacts — {job_id, status, output_dir, files: [{name, kind, path, size_bytes}]}.
- GET /v1/jobs/{id}/artifacts/{name} — downloads; 200 binary with content-type; 404 not_found / artifact_not_found / artifact_missing.
- POST /v1/jobs/{id}/cancel — 200 {job_id, status, cancel_requested, status_url}; 404 unknown.
- GET /healthz — {status: "ok"}.
- GET /readyz — 200 {ready: true, checks} or 503 {ready: false, checks}.

Artifact kinds: step, iso_png, section_png, report, status, console_log, job_metadata, service_metadata, artifact.

report_path points to a report JSON. Report schema: schema_version, job_id, producer, input {path, stem, size_bytes, sha256}, output_dir, reconstruction {status, reason, stats, shape}, validation {reimport_ok, solids, shells, volume, error}, rendering {status: pending|done|failed}, artifacts, steps, timestamps {created_at_utc, finished_at_utc}.

```json
{
  "content": "# CLIENT_PROMPT.md — Build a client for fcadserve\n\nUse this document to create a client library, CLI, or script that talks to a\n**running instance of the `fcadserve` service** — a FreeCAD STL → STEP\nconversion service. The service is assumed to be already deployed and reachable\nover plain HTTPS or HTTP at some base URL you are handed (default dev binding is\n`http://127.0.0.1:8688`; a real deployment will have its own host/port, possibly\nbehind a reverse proxy — always use the base URL you are given).\n\n---\n\n## 1. What the service does\n\nSubmit an **STL file path that lives on the server** and get back:\n\n- a STEP file (`.stp`) converted from the mesh,\n- isometric + cutaway PNG renders of the reconstructed solid,\n- a JSON report describing reconstruction/validation.\n\nThe service is **not** a file upload endpoint: `stl_path` is an absolute path\n**on the server's filesystem** which must be inside an administer-configured\nallowed root. You must know/agree on such a path and a writable output\ndirectory **on the server** before submitting.\n\nTwo-phase processing happens asynchronously: submit → poll → collect.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Conventions\n\n- All bodies and responses are JSON (`application/json`), except artifact\n  downloads and `/healthz`/`/readyz`.\n- **No authentication is built in.** If the deployment adds some, your\n  credentials/headers will be supplied separately.\n- All IDs are opaque strings (`j_…`). Treat them as opaque.\n- Path-relative URLs returned by the service (e.g. `status_url`) are relative\n  to the service base URL — join them to the base you were given; do not\n  re-derive them.\n- There is no API versioning beyond the `/v1` path prefix.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Endpoints\n\n### 3.1 `POST /v1/jobs` — submit a job\n\nRequest body (either spelling below is accepted; mixed is fine):\n\n```json\n{\n  \"stl_path\": \"/server/data/part.stl\",   // snake_case\n  \"output_dir\": \"/server/data/out\"       // optional\n}\n```\n\n```json\n{\n  \"stlPath\": \"/server/data/part.stl\",    // camelCase equivalent\n  \"outputDir\": \"/server/data/out\"\n}\n```\n\nRules:\n- `stl_path` is required and must be **absolute**. `output_dir` is optional;\n  when omitted the STEP/PNGs/report are written next to the input STL. Output\n  dir (explicit or derived) must also be inside an allowed root (or the input's\n  parent when no output roots are configured).\n- Providing the same key twice, or both spellings of the same key\n  (e.g. both `stl_path` and `stlPath`), is rejected as a malformed body.\n- A body that is not a JSON object is rejected.\n\nSuccess — `202 Accepted`:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"job_id\": \"j_1a2b3c4d\",\n  \"status\": \"queued\",\n  \"status_url\": \"/v1/jobs/j_1a2b3c4d\"\n}\n```\n\nErrors — non-2xx with `{ \"code\": \"...\", \"error\": \"...\" }`:\n\n| HTTP | `code` | Meaning / when it happens |\n|------|--------|---------------------------|\n| 400 | `invalid_json` | body missing/not JSON |\n| 400 | `missing_stl_path` | `stl_path` absent or empty |\n| 400 | `relative_path` | `stl_path` (or non-empty `output_dir`) is not absolute |\n| 400 | `unsupported_extension` | input not `.stl` |\n| 400 | `missing_file` | input path does not exist on the server |\n| 400 | `input_is_directory` | input path is a directory |\n| 400 | `input_outside_allowed_root` | input not under a configured input root |\n| 400 | `input_too_large` | exceeds `MaxInputSizeBytes` (default 50 MiB) |\n| 400 | `output_is_file` | `output_dir` is an existing file |\n| 400 | `output_outside_allowed_root` | output dir outside allowed output roots |\n| 400 | `invalid_output_dir` | output dir could not be derived |\n| 409 | `output_already_exists` | a target artifact (`*.stp`, `*_iso.png`, `*_section.png`, `*.report.json`) already exists in the output dir |\n| 409 | `artifact_collides_with_input` | a derived artifact name equals the input path (pathology) |\n| 429 | `queue_full` | too many jobs queued; retry later with backoff |\n\n> `409 output_already_exists` is the **deliberate no-overwrite guard**. To\n> re-run against the same output dir you must first clear the existing\n> artifacts or use a different output dir. There is no client-visible overwrite\n> flag on submission.\n\n### 3.2 `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}` — poll job status\n\n```json\n{\n  \"job_id\": \"j_1a2b3c4d\",\n  \"status\": \"skipped\",\n  \"phase\": \"finished\",\n  \"created_at\": \"2026-09-12T20:51:11.93+00:00\",\n  \"started_at\": \"2026-09-12T20:51:11.99+00:00\",\n  \"finished_at\": \"2026-09-12T20:51:20.60+00:00\",\n  \"updated_at\": \"2026-09-12T20:51:20.60+00:00\",\n  \"stl_path\": \"/server/data/part.stl\",\n  \"output_dir\": \"/server/data/out\",\n  \"effective_output_dir\": \"/server/data/out\",\n  \"input_size_bytes\": 684,\n  \"input_sha256\": \"7f1782bd...\",\n  \"exit_code\": 0,\n  \"error_code\": null,\n  \"error\": null,\n  \"report_path\": \"/server/data/out/part.report.json\",\n  \"artifacts\": [\n    { \"name\": \"part.stp\",       \"kind\": \"step\",        \"path\": \"/server/data/out/part.stp\",               \"size_bytes\": 8412 },\n    { \"name\": \"part_iso.png\",   \"kind\": \"iso_png\",     \"path\": \"/server/data/out/part_iso.png\",           \"size_bytes\": 12043 },\n    { \"name\": \"part_section.png\",\"kind\": \"section_png\",\"path\": \"/server/data/out/part_section.png\",       \"size_bytes\": 10538 },\n    { \"name\": \"part.report.json\",\"kind\": \"report\",     \"path\": \"/server/data/out/part.report.json\",       \"size_bytes\": 2394 }\n  ],\n  \"status_url\": \"/v1/jobs/j_1a2b3c4d\"\n}\n```\n\n- `status` ∈ `queued | running | succeeded | skipped | failed | cancelled`.\n- `phase` ∈ `queued | startingfreecad | loadingstl | reconstructing | exportingstep | validatestep | rendering | writingreport | finished` (informational only; do not gate on it).\n- `exit_code` is the raw FreeCAD process exit code (may be null).\n- `error_code`/`error` are set only on failure/cancellation.\n- `artifacts` may also include sidecars: `status` (worker status.json),\n  `console_log` (FreeCAD console), `job_metadata`.\n- `report_path` points at the JSON report file **on the server** (see §5).\n\n`404` → `{ \"code\": \"not_found\", ... }`.\n\n### 3.3 `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/artifacts` — artifact manifest\n\n```json\n{\n  \"job_id\": \"j_1a2b3c4d\",\n  \"status\": \"skipped\",\n  \"output_dir\": \"/server/data/out\",\n  \"files\": [\n    { \"name\": \"part.stp\", \"kind\": \"step\", \"path\": \"/server/data/out/part.stp\", \"size_bytes\": 8412 },\n    { \"name\": \"part_iso.png\", \"kind\": \"iso_png\", \"path\": \"/server/data/out/part_iso.png\", \"size_bytes\": 12043 }\n  ]\n}\n```\n\n`kind` is one of `step | iso_png | section_png | report | status |\nconsole_log | job_metadata | service_metadata | artifact` (unknown/derived names\nclassify as `artifact`).\n\n### 3.4 `GET /v1/jobs/{jobId}/artifacts/{name}` — download artifact\n\nBinary download (server reads the file and streams):\n\n- `200` with `Content-Type`: `image/png`, `application/step`,\n  `application/json`, `text/plain`, else `application/octet-stream`.\n- `404` → `{ \"code\": \"artifact_not_found\" | \"artifact_missing\", ... }`.\n\nUse `{name}` exactly as listed in the manifest (`part.stp`,\n`part_iso.png`, etc.).\n\n### 3.5 `POST /v1/jobs/{jobId}/cancel` — cancel a job\n\n```json\n{\n  \"job_id\": \"j_1a2b3c4d\",\n  \"status\": \"cancelled\",\n  \"cancel_requested\": true,\n  \"status_url\": \"/v1/jobs/j_1a2b3c4d\"\n}\n```\n\n- Cancelling a **queued** job flips it straight to `cancelled`.\n- Cancelling a **running** job sets `cancel_requested: true`, kills the worker\n  process group, and the job settles to `cancelled` shortly after.\n- Cancelling an already-terminal job is a no-op (`cancel_requested` reflects\n  whether a cancel was acted on).\n- `404` for an unknown job.\n\n### 3.6 `GET /healthz` — liveness\n\n`200` `{ \"status\": \"ok\" }`. Does not check dependencies (FreeCAD, Xvfb, DB).\n\n### 3.7 `GET /readyz` — readiness\n\n- `200` `{ \"ready\": true, \"checks\": { … } }` when FreeCAD entrypoint, Xvfb,\n  worker script, DB, state root and input roots are all usable.\n- `503` `{ \"ready\": false, \"checks\": { … } }` otherwise (e.g. still warming up,\n  input roots misconfigured).\n\nClient guidance: **wait for `ready:true` before submitting the first job** and\nretry with backoff on `503`.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Job lifecycle & what counts as \"done\"\n\n```\nsubmit ─▶ queued ─▶ running ─▶ succeeded | skipped | failed\n                  │               └─ cancel ─▶ cancelled\n                  └─ cancel (queued) ─▶ cancelled\n```\n\nTerminal statuses (once reached the job won't change):\n- **`succeeded`** — reconstruction produced a valid analytic solid; STEP,\n  renders and report published.\n- **`skipped`** — **successful fallback**. The mesher could not build a clean\n  analytic solid, so the pipeline published the usable/partial result anyway\n  (raw geometry was insufficient for a watertight solid, etc.). Artifacts are\n  still published and downloadable. **Treat `skipped` as success**, though the\n  `validation` section may report `solids: 0`. See §5.\n- **`failed`** — processing stopped; `error_code`/`error` explain why. Common\n  codes seen at the job level: `missing_status`, `worker_failed`,\n  `reconstruct_error`, `invalid_step_reimport`, `export_step_error`,\n  `load_stl_error`, `render_failed`, `missing_render_result`,\n  `missing_artifacts`, `job_timeout`, `freecad_start_failed`, `xvfb_failed`,\n  `cancelled`/`render_timeout`/`internal_error`.\n- **`cancelled`** — explicitly cancelled.\n\nThere is **no server-side webhook**; clients must poll `GET /v1/jobs/{id}`.\n\n---\n\n## 5. The report file (JSON, downloaded like any artifact)\n\n`report_path` (or the `report` artifact) is a JSON document:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"schema_version\": 1,\n  \"job_id\": \"j_1a2b3c4d\",\n  \"producer\": { \"software\": \"FreeCAD\", \"version\": [\"1\",\"1\",\"3\",\"\"], \"reconstructor\": \"fcadserve.py\" },\n  \"input\": { \"path\": \"/server/data/part.stl\", \"stem\": \"part\", \"size_bytes\": 684,\n             \"sha256\": \"7f1782bd...\" },\n  \"output_dir\": \"\",\n  \"reconstruction\": { \"status\": \"skipped\", \"reason\": \"...\", \"stats\": {}, \"shape\": { \"volume\": 27000.0, \"shells\": 1, ... } },\n  \"validation\":   { \"reimport_ok\": true, \"solids\": 0, \"shells\": 1, \"volume\": 27000.0, \"error\": null },\n  \"rendering\":    { \"status\": \"done\" },\n  \"artifacts\":    { \"step\": \"part.stp\", \"iso_png\": \"part_iso.png\", \"section_png\": \"part_section.png\", \"section_unavailable_reason\": null },\n  \"steps\": [ { \"name\": \"load_stl\", \"status\": \"ok\", \"started_at_utc\": \"...\", \"duration_ms\": 4 }, ... ],\n  \"timestamps\":  { \"created_at_utc\": \"...\", \"finished_at_utc\": \"...\" }\n}\n```\n\n- `input.sha256` == `input_sha256` in the job view == the DB value.\n- `reconstruction.status` mirrors the job status (`reconstructed` /\n  `skipped`). When `skipped`, `reason` explains the fallback.\n- `validation.reimport_ok` tells you the STEP round-tripped.\n- `rendering.status` becomes `done` after the render phase.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Recommended client behavior (skeleton)\n\n1. **Base + readiness** — remember your base URL. Loop `GET /readyz` until\n   `ready:true` (backoff, e.g. 1s → 2s → 4s, cap ~15s; give up after a timeout\n   you choose, e.g. 90s).\n2. **Submit** — `POST /v1/jobs` with an absolute server-side `stl_path`\n   (+ optional `output_dir`). Expect `202`. Read `job_id` and **use the\n   returned `status_url`** for polling.\n3. **Handle submission errors**:\n   - `409 output_already_exists` → tell the caller to pick another output dir\n     or clear the target artifacts first.\n   - `429 queue_full` → exponential backoff and retry the submit.\n   - other 400s → permanent client/business errors; surface `code` + `error`.\n4. **Poll** — `GET /v1/jobs/{job_id}` every 1–3 s until `status` is terminal\n   (`succeeded|skipped|failed|cancelled`). Add a server-side timeout only if\n   you know the deployment's `TimeoutSeconds` (default 600 s); the service\n   fails its own timeouts with `error_code: job_timeout`.\n5. **Terminal handling** — `succeeded` and `skipped` are both success; check\n   `error_code`/`error` for `failed`. For success, fetch the artifact manifest\n   (`GET .../artifacts`), then download what you need by name. `kind == \"step\"`\n   is the deliverable; `iso_png`/`section_png` are the renders; `report` is the\n   provenance document.\n6. **Cancel** — `POST .../cancel` when shutting down or a caller aborts;\n   then poll to a terminal `cancelled` state.\n7. **Retries** — treat `5xx`/network failures as transient; never blindly\n   resubmit on `409`.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Build-verification checklist\n\nA complete client:\n- [ ] polls `/readyz` before submitting\n- [ ] can submit using the documented snake_case body verbatim\n- [ ] also accepts/emits camelCase without breaking\n- [ ] surfaces every documented error `code` distinctly\n- [ ] treats `skipped` as success and exposes `validation`/`reason`\n- [ ] polls until terminal, uses returned `status_url`\n- [ ] lists + downloads artifacts by manifest name\n- [ ] handles cancel for queued and running jobs\n- [ ] handles `409` (naming collision) as a *user-visible, non-retryable* error\n- [ ] demonstrates capturing the report and checking `input.sha256`\n\nA good smoke test against a live instance (curl):\n\n```bash\nBASE=http://127.0.0.1:8688\ncurl -sf $BASE/healthz\ncurl -sf $BASE/readyz\n\n# after you confirm an STL exists on the host + an allowed output dir:\nresp=$(curl -s -X POST $BASE/v1/jobs \\\n  -H 'content-type: application/json' \\\n  -d '{\"stl_path\":\"/server/data/part.stl\",\"output_dir\":\"/server/data/out\"}')\njob=$(echo \"$resp\" | jq -r .job_id)\nuntil curl -s $BASE/v1/jobs/$job | jq -e '.status == \"succeeded\" or .status == \"skipped\" or .status == \"failed\"' >/dev/null; do sleep 2; done\ncurl -s $BASE/v1/jobs/$job | jq\ncurl -sO $BASE/v1/jobs/$job/artifacts/part.stp\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. Gotchas\n\n- **Server-side paths only.** `stl_path`/`output_dir` refer to the machine\n  running fcadserve, not your local machine. There is no upload endpoint.\n- **Path understanding is security-relevant.** A `404`-free submit that\n  returns `input_outside_allowed_root`/`output_outside_allowed_root` means the\n  path is invalid *for that deployment* — ask the operator for the configured\n  roots (`/readyz` `checks.input_roots` shows them).\n- **No overwrite.** Re-running is expected to fail with `409` until the output\n  dir is cleared — by design.\n- **`skipped` ≠ error.** Many inputs (especially low-poly or highly tessellated\n  meshes) land in the analytic-fallback path; artifacts are still valid output.\n- **Artifact names are deterministic** from the input stem\n  (`<stem>.stp`, `<stem>_iso.png`, `<stem>_section.png`, `<stem>.report.json`),\n  but always read the manifest rather than guessing; `section_png` can be\n  absent when a section cannot be produced.\n- **Phase is coarse** (`startingfreecad`, `loadingstl`, … all-lowercase) and\n  informational; gate on `status`.\n- **Statuses are lowercased enum names.** Compare case-insensitively if your\n  client normalizes.\n- **camelCase vs snake_case:** do not send both spellings of a key in one body.",
  "filePath": "/home/fat64/github/FreeCADService/CLIENT_PROMPT.md"

## Assistant (Build · Big Pickle · 15.8s)

_Thinking:_

The user says someone is ready to connect to our server. We need to prepare before launching. Let me think about what's needed to actually deploy/launch the service so an external party can connect.

Key considerations:

1. **Network exposure**: service binds 127.0.0.1:8688 by default. Someone external connecting means we need to bind to a network interface (or use a reverse proxy). Need to decide: direct bind vs reverse proxy (nginx/caddy) for TLS.

2. **The service needs actual STL files on the server** — the external party will submit `stl_path`/`output_dir` that reference server-side paths. So the operator must configure:
   - `FCADSERVE_ALLOWED_INPUT_ROOTS` — where input STLs may live
   - `FCADSERVE_ALLOWED_OUTPUT_ROOTS` — where outputs may be written
   - How are they going to transfer STLs to the server (upload)? Or do they expect the service to accept uploads? Currently there's NO upload endpoint. This is a critical gap — if the client is told to submit `stl_path` as a server path, they need a way to GET the STL onto the server. Need to clarify: does the other party have filesystem access? Or do we need an upload mechanism? This is the biggest open question.

3. **Requirements on the server**: 
   - dotnet 10 runtime
   - FreeCAD flatpak (org.freecad.FreeCAD) installed
   - Xvfb installed
   - STL files for testing (a test STL)
   - writable state root (/var/lib/fcadserve)

4. **Package/deploy**: currently dev build in src/fcadserve/bin/Debug. For production we'd want `dotnet publish -c Release` and place scripts (freecad/*.py), service binary somewhere stable (/usr/lib/fcadserve), service unit install, set up state dirs with correct permissions (non-root user), env file.

5. **TLS/auth**: currently no auth. Anyone who can reach the endpoint can trigger FreeCAD runs (resource consumption, write to allowed roots). Should we put it behind TLS + basic auth or API token? At minimum, should we require it? For a first external connect, maybe a simple static header token. This is a decision.

6. **Operational verification**: run the service under systemd, verify readyz ready, verify an end-to-end job, open firewall port, provide base URL + any creds, ensure security (bind address, no overwrite, size limits).

7. **The `work/` scratch STL** — we need a real input STL at a stable location in an allowed root (e.g. /var/lib/fcadserve or /srv/fcadserve/input). Generate a test STL.

8. **Documentation for the connector**: point them at CLIENT_PROMPT.md.

9. **Base URL behind reverse proxy**: status_url behavior uses PathBase; if we put behind a subpath proxy, client must use that base. Simplest: direct bind or root path proxy.

Let me also consider: the external party "connect" — do they connect from another machine? Presumably internet/network. So we need networking. If the machine is behind a firewall/NAT, we may need port forwarding or a tunnel.

- How will the client deliver STL files (upload endpoint needed? scp? shared filesystem?)

- Public vs internal network

1: # fcadserve — FreeCAD STL -> STEP conversion HTTP service.
2: #
3: # Install (as root):
4: #   install -d -o fcadserve -g fcadserve /var/lib/fcadserve
5: #   install -o root -g root deploy/fcadserve.service /etc/systemd/system/fcadserve.service
6: #   systemctl daemon-reload && systemctl enable --now fcadserve
7: #
8: # Configuration comes from /etc/fcadserve/fcadserve.conf (EnvironmentFile).
9: # At minimum set:
10: #   FCADSERVE_ALLOWED_INPUT_ROOTS=/data/stl
11: #   FCADSERVE_ALLOWED_OUTPUT_ROOTS=/data/stl
12: # and optionally FCADSERVE_STATE_ROOT, FCADSERVE_PORT, etc.
13: #
14: # Hardening notes:
15: #  - ProtectSystem=strict makes every path read-only except ReadWritePaths.
16: #    The flatpak sandbox maps host paths for FreeCAD independently, so
17: #    write access for the flatpak process follows the HOST paths granted
18: #    here (state dir) plus anything granted to flatpak by the service user.
19: #  - NoNewPrivileges=true is safe for flatpak/Xvfb (they do not raise
20: #    privileges) and neutral for .NET.
21: #  - MemoryDenyWriteExecute/SystemCallFilter are intentionally NOT set:
22: #    .NET's JIT needs writable+executable mappings and flatpak uses several
23: #    syscalls that a strict filter would break.
24: #  - FreeCAD/Xvfb are spawned in their own setsid process groups but remain
25: #    in this unit's cgroup, so the default KillMode=control-group removes
26: #    them on stop/restart. TimeoutStopSec gives the graceful shutdown path
27: #    time to kill active workers and their X displays first.
28: 
29: [Unit]
30: Description=fcadserve FreeCAD conversion service
31: Documentation=file:///usr/lib/fcadserve/README.md
32: After=network.target
33: 
34: [Service]
35: Type=simple
36: User=fcadserve
37: Group=fcadserve
38: WorkingDirectory=/usr/lib/fcadserve
39: EnvironmentFile=-/etc/fcadserve/fcadserve.conf
40: ExecStart=/usr/share/dotnet/dotnet /usr/lib/fcadserve/fcadserve.dll
41: Restart=on-failure
42: RestartSec=3
43: TimeoutStopSec=30
44: KillMode=control-group
45: 
46: NoNewPrivileges=true
47: ProtectSystem=strict
48: ReadWritePaths=/var/lib/fcadserve
49: ProtectHome=true
50: PrivateTmp=true
51: ProtectKernelTunables=true
52: ProtectKernelModules=true
53: ProtectClock=true
54: LockPersonality=true
55: RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6
56: RestrictRealtime=true
57: SystemCallArchitectures=native
58: 
59: [Install]
60: WantedBy=multi-user.target

(End of file - total 60 lines)
</content>
```

Decisions:
1. Add an upload endpoint (POST /v1/uploads) that stores STL into a staging root, then submit jobs against it.

Now let me plan the work.

## Work plan

### A. Upload endpoint
- New endpoint `POST /v1/uploads` (multipart or raw binary). Let me design:
  - Raw binary body with filename determined by client? Or multipart/form-data with file field + optional target name?
  - Simplest robust approach: `multipart/form-data` with a `file` part. Or raw body + `Content-Disposition` filename. 
  - I'd go with raw binary PUT-style: `POST /v1/uploads` with `Content-Type: application/octet-stream` (or `.stl`) and a header/query param for the filename, OR derive filename from `Content-Disposition`. Use a query param `?name=part.stl`. Store into staging root (a configured `FCADSERVE_UPLOAD_ROOT`, default under state root like `/var/lib/fcadserve/uploads`), with collision-safe unique naming.
  - Response: 201 with `{ upload_id, stl_path, name, size_bytes, sha256, status_url? }`. Then client submits `{ "stl_path": "<uploaded stl_path>" }`.
  - Security/size limits: enforce `MaxInputSizeBytes` (same limit), only `.stl` extension, sanitize filename (no path traversal), generate unique names (uploadId + sanitized name) to avoid collisions.
  - Content: options — RAW body (POST /v1/uploads with octet-stream) is easiest for curl (`-H 'content-type: application/octet-stream' --data-binary @part.stl`). But filename must come from somewhere: `?name=` query or `Content-Disposition`. Multipart is more standard but clunkier for simple curl. Since our client is a custom agent-built client, raw body + `?name=` query param + optional `Content-Disposition` fallback gives flexibility. Let me support both: prefer `Content-Disposition` filename, else `name` query param, else reject 400 missing_filename.
  - STL extension check: name must end in .stl (case-insensitive).
  - Uploaded staging dir must be made an input root so submit passes `input_outside_allowed_root`. Options: (1) add the upload root to AllowedInputRoots at startup automatically, or (2) expose upload root as a separate config and have the submit path special-case it. Cleanest: at startup, append `UploadRoot` to the input roots list (it's a valid input root regardless), OR make PathPolicy aware. Simpler: when building ServiceOptions, register upload root inside allowed input roots. Let me check how allowed roots are wired.
  - size limit enforcement before writing to disk: stream with limited buffer, count bytes while copying; abort if exceeds MaxInputSizeBytes → 413 (or 400 input_too_large). Actually we can enforce body size via Kestrel RequestSizeLimit per-endpoint, but simpler to count while streaming and delete on exceed, return 400 `input_too_large`.
  - Also enforce the sha256 to be computed for response (already have pattern from submit — input_sha256 computed maybe. Actually sha256 computed in worker report, not at submit. For upload response, computing sha256 is useful; we can hash while writing (SHA256 incremental). Cheap since we stream anyway.)

- Should uploads automatically trigger a job or just stage? Recommended behavior: stage + return path; client then submits a job referencing that path. Two-step keeps it composable and lets client choose output_dir. The upload root is auto an input root, but output dir: client can pass output_dir; if omitted, output written next to input (inside upload root). That's fine.

Actually, could auto-submit after upload (single round trip)? Two-step is more flexible and matches existing submit endpoint. Keep two-step.

### B. Config additions
- `FCADSERVE_UPLOAD_ROOT` (default: `<StateRoot>/uploads`, or `/var/lib/fcadserve/uploads`).
- Ensure state root gets created at startup if missing (JobDatabase creates it? check).
- Bind address: `FCADSERVE_URLS`/ASPNETCORE_URLS or a `BindAddress` config. Currently Program binds `http://127.0.0.1:8688`. Need config `FCADSERVE_BIND_ADDRESS=0.0.0.0` or `192.168.1.66`, or just `FCADSERVE_URLS=http://0.0.0.0:8688`. Let me check Program.cs to see how the Kestrel bind is set. Simplest: honor `ASPNETCORE_URLS` env (standard). But Chinese? no. Detect collision with existing `--Port` CLI. Let me check Program.cs bind.

### C. Deployment on this machine
- `dotnet publish -c Release -o <publish>` for fcadserve.
- Install layout under /usr/lib/fcadserve (needs sudo/root): binary, freecad/*.py scripts, README, GENESIS prompts? Keep: fcadserve.dll + deps + freecad/ + CLIENT_PROMPT.md/README.
- Create user fcadserve, /var/lib/fcadserve (state, uploads), /etc/fcadserve/fcadserve.conf with LAN binding + allowed roots (e.g. /var/lib/fcadserve/uploads as both input & output? configure inputs = uploads root; outputs = /var/lib/fcadserve/data or uploads too).
- FreeCAD flatpak access: system-wide install already present; service user must be able to run `flatpak run --branch=stable --arch=x86_64 org.freecad.FreeCAD ...`. flatpak system app is available to all users? System-wide flatpak apps: yes typically runnable by any user (with appropriate permission). The worker uses `flatpak run`. Should work for user fcadserve. Xvfb for headless display.
- Also `ProtectHome=true` in systemd — fine. `PrivateTmp=true` — the worker writes to state root under /var/lib/fcadserve (ReadWritePaths covers it). But FreeCAD flatpak writes are redirected into the host sandbox — with the sandbox mapping host paths; the env var FCADSERVE_STATE_ROOT must map. Our worker already ran under systemd? We've only run it manually so far. This is the first real systemd deployment. Watch for flatpak-in-systemd issues (systemd user services, dbus). flatpak system apps use the system bus; should be OK.
  - Concern: `ProtectSystem=strict` + flatpak writes — worker operates within state root (host path) which is ReadWritePaths. FreeCAD CLI output (step/png/report/status) goes under state root → writable. Good.
  - Xvfb: unit doesn't spawn Xvfb; the worker starts its own Xvfb (Xvfb :N -screen ...). Under `PrivateTmp`/`RestrictAddressFamilies=AF_UNIX AF_INET AF_INET6` — Xvfb needs /tmp/.X11-unix sockets (AF_UNIX ok) — but with PrivateTmp=true, /tmp/.X11-unix is a separate mount? Actually /tmp/.X11-unix is not under /tmp for X sockets — it's tmpfs mounted separately; PrivateTmp may remap. Hmm, Xvfb default socket dir is /tmp/.X11-unix. With PrivateTmp=true, the unit's /tmp is a private dir, and /tmp/.X11-unix would resolve inside the private tmp, whereas X clients look in /tmp/.X11-unix (the real /tmp) inside their own private namespace... Both worker (invoking Xvfb with -display) and the FreeCAD process need consistent X display. The worker runs FreeCAD and Xvfb under the same unit cgroup and same mount namespace (same private tmp). Xvfb creates /tmp/.X11-unix/X{n} inside the private tmp; FreeCAD spawned by worker inherits the mount namespace and looks in /tmp/.X11-unix → finds it. GUI mode uses DISPLAY var. So consistent. But wait — flatpak run launches the app in its OWN mount namespace (bubblewrap). The flatpak sandbox remaps host paths; does it see the private /tmp? flatpak's default filesystem includes /tmp? By default flatpak apps see a private /tmp? Actually flatpak apps do NOT get host /tmp by default? The `--filesystem` includes: home, /tmp? Let me recall: flatpak default sandbox includes `~`, /proc, /dev, /sys, and a private /tmp shared via... Actually recent flatpak: apps get `/tmp` but writes redirected? Hmm — there have been changes: flatpak historically provided a shared /tmp; newer versions may give private /tmp per-app. This is a real deployment risk with PrivateTmp=true + flatpak GUI render + Xvfb socket pass-through.

This is getting deep. The pragmatic call: the e2e we already ran worked under manual launch (not systemd, no PrivateTmp). Under systemd PrivateTmp=true, the Xvfb socket in the service's private /tmp may not be visible to the flatpak app's namespace. Known workaround: Xvfb with `-shm`? no. Some set XAUTHORITY and use `--serve`... The X socket path problem: set Xvfb `-nolisten`? The real issue is the .X11-unix dir. Options:
  - Set PrivateTmp=false for the unit (simpler, workspace=/var/lib/fcadserve for worker scratch is under ReadWritePaths anyway). Loosen security slightly but fine on trusted LAN.
  - Or configure Xvfb socketdir elsewhere via `-display` and set `DISPLAY` consistently, and give flatpak `--socket=x11` mapping /tmp/.X11-unix into sandbox. flatpak's `x11` socket feature maps the shared X11 socket dir into sandbox regardless of private tmp? The `--socket=x11` grants access to the host X11 sockets, not the bubblewrap-internal ones.

Empirically test during deploy: after installing unit, POST an upload + job, confirm render PNG produced (rendering done) and step exists. If render fails → diagnose (likely flatpak/private-tmp/X socket). Fallback: set PrivateTmp=false in unit. Also may need `Environment=XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` for flatpak? flatpak run as non-interactive service user needs a valid XDG_RUNTIME_DIR; systemd sets it? With `PAMName`? For system services, XDG_RUNTIME_DIR unset. flatpak warns but still runs? We should set `Environment=XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/fcadserve` (mkdir with 0700 by fcadserve user; systemd can RuntimeDirectory=fcadserve → /run/fcadserve).

Also flatpak requires `dbus-system` (system bus). RestrictAddressFamilies AF_UNIX ok; but flatpak needs `--system-talk-name`? flatpak run itself (the launcher) talks to flatpak system daemon via /run/flatpak.sock? or dbus. Should be OK but verify at runtime; we already ran flatpak run subtly.

Also `GitHub`? no.

Also `SystemCallArchitectures=native` fine.

14:         v1.MapPost("/jobs", async (SubmitJobRequest? req, JobDirector director, HttpContext ctx, CancellationToken ct) =>

17:                 return Results.BadRequest(Error("invalid_json", "request body must be JSON."));
18: 
19:             try
20:             {
21:                 var job = await director.SubmitAsync(req.StlPath, req.OutputDir, ct);

<content>
1: using System.Threading.Channels;
2: using FcadServe.FreeCad;
3: using FcadServe.Options;
4: using FcadServe.Security;
5: 
6: namespace FcadServe.Jobs;
7: 
8: public sealed class JobQueueFullException : Exception
9: {
10:     public JobQueueFullException() : base("job queue is full.") { }
11: }
12: 
13: /// <summary>
14: /// Owns the job queue and handles submission, cancellation, restart recovery
15: /// and retention pruning. Never executes FreeCAD geometry itself.
16: /// </summary>
17: public sealed class JobDirector(
18:     Channel<JobRecord> queue,
19:     JobStore store,
20:     JobDatabase db,
21:     PathPolicy policy,
22:     ActiveProcessRegistry registry,
23:     ILogger<JobDirector> logger) : BackgroundService
24: {
25:     private readonly PeriodicTimer _pruneTimer = new(TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5));
26: 
27:     /// <summary>Validates and queues a new job. Returns the created record.</summary>
28:     public async Task<JobRecord> SubmitAsync(string? stlPath, string? outputDir, CancellationToken ct = default)
29:     {
30:         var (input, output) = policy.Resolve(stlPath, outputDir);
31: 
32:         var id = store.CreateJobId();
33:         var requested = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(outputDir) ? null : outputDir;
34:         var job = await store.CreateAsync(id, input.StlPath, requested, output.Directory, input.SizeBytes, input.Sha256, ct);
35: 
36:         if (!queue.Writer.TryWrite(job))
37:         {
38:             await db.DeleteAsync(id, ct);
39:             throw new JobQueueFullException();
40:         }
41:         return job;
42:     }
43: 
44:     /// <summary>Cancels a queued or running job. Returns false if the job id is unknown.</summary>
45:     public async Task<bool> CancelAsync(string id, CancellationToken ct = default)
46:     {
47:         var job = await store.GetAsync(id, ct);
48:         if (job is null)
49:             return false;
50:         if (job.IsTerminal)
51:             return true;
52: 
53:         // Signal first so the worker sees the flag even if the group kill races.
54:         await store.RequestCancelAsync(id, ct);
55:         if (registry.TryGetPid(id, out var pid))
56:         {
57:             await ProcessGroupKiller.KillGroupAsync(pid, ct);
58:             logger.LogInformation("Job {JobId}: killed worker process group {Pid}", id, pid);
59:         }
60:         return true;
61:     }
62: 
63:     protected override async Task ExecuteAsync(CancellationToken stoppingToken)
64:     {
65:         while (await _pruneTimer.WaitForNextTickAsync(stoppingToken))
66:         {
67:             try
68:             {
69:                 await store.PruneAsync(stoppingToken);
70:             }
71:             catch (OperationCanceledException)
72:             {
73:                 break;
74:             }
75:             catch (Exception ex)
76:             {
77:                 logger.LogError(ex, "Prune pass failed");
78:             }
79:         }
80:     }
81: 
82:     public override async Task StartAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken)
83:     {
84:         // Database init + restart recovery must finish before queue workers
85:         // drain the channel (hosted services start in registration order, and
86:         // the worker is registered after this service).
87:         await db.InitializeAsync(cancellationToken);
88:         await base.StartAsync(cancellationToken);
89: 
90:         var recovered = await store.RecoverOrphansAsync(cancellationToken);
91:         foreach (var job in recovered)
92:         {
93:             if (!queue.Writer.TryWrite(job))
94:             {
95:                 logger.LogWarning("Job {JobId}: recovered job could not be re-queued (queue full)", job.Id);
96:             }
97:         }
98:         await store.PruneAsync(cancellationToken);
99:     }
100: }
101: 
102: /// <summary>Worker pool: drains the job queue with up to WorkerCount concurrent executors.</summary>
103: public sealed class JobQueueWorker(
104:     Channel<JobRecord> queue,
105:     JobExecutor executor,
106:     ServiceOptions options,
107:     ILogger<JobQueueWorker> logger) : BackgroundService
108: {
109:     protected override async Task ExecuteAsync(CancellationToken stoppingToken)
110:     {
111:         var count = Math.Max(1, options.Jobs.WorkerCount);
112:         var tasks = new Task[count];
113:         for (var i = 0; i < count; i++)
114:         {
115:             var worker = i;
116:             tasks[i] = Task.Run(() => WorkerLoopAsync(worker, stoppingToken), CancellationToken.None);
117:         }
118:         await Task.WhenAll(tasks).ConfigureAwait(false);
119:     }
120: 
121:     private async Task WorkerLoopAsync(int worker, CancellationToken ct)
122:     {
123:         await foreach (var job in queue.Reader.ReadAllAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false))
124:         {

1: namespace FcadServe.Security;

3: /// <summary>Thrown when job input/output validation fails.</summary>
4: public sealed class PathValidationException(string code, string message) : Exception(message)
5: {
6:     public string Code { get; } = code;
7: }
8: 
9: public static class PathUtils
10: {
11:     /// <summary>Parses ";" and "," separated root lists from configuration.</summary>
12:     public static string[] SplitList(string[]? values)
13:     {
14:         if (values is null or { Length: 0 })
15:             return [];
16:         return values
17:             .SelectMany(v => v.Split([';', ','], StringSplitOptions.RemoveEmptyEntries | StringSplitOptions.TrimEntries))
18:             .Distinct(StringComparer.Ordinal)
19:             .ToArray();
20:     }
21: 
22:     public static bool IsWithin(string path, string root)
23:     {
24:         var p = Path.TrimEndingDirectorySeparator(Path.GetFullPath(path));
25:         var r = Path.TrimEndingDirectorySeparator(Path.GetFullPath(root));
26:         if (string.Equals(p, r, StringComparison.Ordinal))
27:             return true;
28:         if (string.Equals(r, Path.GetPathRoot(r), StringComparison.Ordinal))
29:             return true; // root of the filesystem contains every path
30:         return p.StartsWith(r + Path.DirectorySeparatorChar, StringComparison.Ordinal);
31:     }
32: 
33:     /// <summary>
34:     /// Resolves symlinks for every existing path component so allowlists cannot
35:     /// be bypassed by links that point outside a configured root. The final
36:     /// component and intermediate directories may not exist yet (output dirs).
37:     /// </summary>
38:     public static string ResolvePath(string path)
39:     {
40:         var full = Path.GetFullPath(path);
41:         var root = Path.GetPathRoot(full) ?? "/";
42:         var rel = full.Length > root.Length ? full[root.Length..] : string.Empty;
43:         var parts = rel.Split(['/', '\\'], StringSplitOptions.RemoveEmptyEntries);
44:         var cur = root;
45:         foreach (var part in parts)
46:         {
47:             cur = Path.Combine(cur, part);
48:             if (!File.Exists(cur) && !Directory.Exists(cur))
49:             {
50:                 // Component does not exist (future output path, or part of one).
51:                 continue;
52:             }
53:             FileSystemInfo info = File.Exists(cur) ? new FileInfo(cur) : new DirectoryInfo(cur);
54:             var target = info.ResolveLinkTarget(returnFinalTarget: true) ?? info;
55:             if (target.FullName != cur)
56:                 cur = target.FullName;
57:         }
58:         return Path.GetFullPath(cur);
59:     }
60: }
61: 
62: public sealed record InputValidation(string StlPath, string ResolvedStlPath, string Stem, long SizeBytes, string Sha256);
63: 
64: public sealed record OutputValidation(
65:     string Directory,
66:     string StepPath,
67:     string IsoPngPath,
68:     string SectionPngPath,
69:     string ReportPath,
70:     bool SectionAvailable);
71: 
72: /// <summary>
73: /// Validates job inputs and outputs against the configured filesystem policy.
74: /// Path handling is treated as a security boundary (see GENESIS spec).
75: /// </summary>
76: public sealed class PathPolicy(Options.ServiceOptions options)
77: {
78:     private static readonly string[] AllowedExtensions = [".stl"];
79: 
80:     private readonly string[] _inputRoots = PathUtils.SplitList(options.AllowedInputRoots);
81:     private readonly string[] _outputRoots = PathUtils.SplitList(options.AllowedOutputRoots);
82:     private readonly string[] _inputRootsResolved = PathUtils.SplitList(options.AllowedInputRoots).Select(PathUtils.ResolvePath).ToArray();
83:     private readonly string[] _outputRootsResolved = PathUtils.SplitList(options.AllowedOutputRoots).Select(PathUtils.ResolvePath).ToArray();
84: 
85:     public IReadOnlyList<string> AllowedInputRoots => _inputRoots;
86:     public IReadOnlyList<string> AllowedOutputRoots => _outputRoots;
87: 
88:     private static bool IsAbsolute(string path) => Path.IsPathFullyQualified(path) && Path.IsPathRooted(path);
89: 
90:     /// <summary>Full validation for job submission: paths, roots, collisions, checksum.</summary>
91:     public (InputValidation Input, OutputValidation Output) Resolve(string? stlPath, string? outputDir)
92:     {
93:         var (input, output) = Plan(stlPath, outputDir);
94: 
95:         // Never overwrite the input STL and avoid clobbering existing artifacts.
96:         if (!options.OverwriteArtifacts)
97:         {
98:             var existing = new[] { output.StepPath, output.IsoPngPath, output.SectionPngPath, output.ReportPath }.Where(File.Exists).ToList();
99:             if (existing.Count > 0)
100:                 throw new PathValidationException("output_already_exists",
101:                     $"target artifact already exists in output directory (use overwrite policy or a different output_dir): {string.Join(", ", existing)}");
102:         }
103: 
104:         var sha256 = ComputeSha256(input.ResolvedStlPath);
105:         return (input with { Sha256 = sha256 }, output);
106:     }
107: 
108:     /// <summary>
109:     /// Validates paths and derives the output layout without side-effect checks
110:     /// (no collision check or checksum). Used by the worker executor, which must
111:     /// not fail just because a destination now exists.
112:     /// </summary>
113:     public (InputValidation Input, OutputValidation Output) Plan(string? stlPath, string? outputDir)
114:     {
115:         if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(stlPath))
116:             throw new PathValidationException("missing_stl_path", "stl_path is required.");
117:         if (!IsAbsolute(stlPath))
118:             throw new PathValidationException("relative_path", "stl_path must be an absolute path.");
119: 
120:         var ext = Path.GetExtension(stlPath);
121:         if (!AllowedExtensions.Contains(ext, StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase))
122:             throw new PathValidationException("unsupported_extension", $"unsupported extension '{ext}' (allowed: .stl).");
123: 
124:         var resolvedInput = PathUtils.ResolvePath(stlPath);
125:         if (!File.Exists(resolvedInput))
126:             throw new PathValidationException("missing_file", $"input file does not exist: {resolvedInput}");
127:         if (Directory.Exists(resolvedInput))
128:             throw new PathValidationException("input_is_directory", "stl_path must point to a file.");
129: 
130:         if (_inputRootsResolved.Length > 0 &&
131:             !_inputRootsResolved.Any(root => PathUtils.IsWithin(resolvedInput, root)))
132:             throw new PathValidationException("input_outside_allowed_root", $"input file is outside the configured allowed input roots.");
133: 
134:         var fi = new FileInfo(resolvedInput);
135:         var size = fi.Length;
136:         if (size > options.Jobs.MaxInputSizeBytes)
137:             throw new PathValidationException("input_too_large",
138:                 $"input file is {size} bytes, exceeding the maximum of {options.Jobs.MaxInputSizeBytes}.");
139: 
140:         // Effective output directory.
141:         string outDir;
142:         if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(outputDir))
143:         {
144:             outDir = Path.GetDirectoryName(resolvedInput)
145:                      ?? throw new PathValidationException("invalid_output_dir", "cannot derive output directory from input path.");
146:         }
147:         else
148:         {
149:             if (!IsAbsolute(outputDir))
150:                 throw new PathValidationException("relative_path", "output_dir must be an absolute path or empty.");
151:             outDir = outputDir;
152:         }
153: 
154:         var resolvedOut = PathUtils.ResolvePath(outDir);
155:         if (File.Exists(resolvedOut))
156:             throw new PathValidationException("output_is_file", "output_dir must be a directory.");
157: 
158:         // Output location policy: must be inside a configured output root, or,
159:         // when none are configured, the input's parent directory.
160:         var inputParent = Path.GetDirectoryName(resolvedInput)!;
161:         var allowedOutputs = _outputRootsResolved.Length > 0
162:             ? _outputRootsResolved
163:             : [PathUtils.ResolvePath(inputParent)];
164: 
165:         if (!allowedOutputs.Any(root => PathUtils.IsWithin(resolvedOut, root)))
166:             throw new PathValidationException("output_outside_allowed_root", "output_dir is outside the configured allowed output roots.");
167: 
168:         // Deterministic stem-based artifact names + unique job manifest.
169:         var stem = Path.GetFileNameWithoutExtension(stlPath);
170:         var step = Path.Combine(resolvedOut, $"{stem}.stp");
171:         var iso = Path.Combine(resolvedOut, $"{stem}_iso.png");
172:         var section = Path.Combine(resolvedOut, $"{stem}_section.png");
173:         var report = Path.Combine(resolvedOut, $"{stem}.report.json");
174: 
175:         // Never overwrite the input STL (name-space collision is inherent to the layout).
176:         if (string.Equals(Path.GetFullPath(step), resolvedInput, StringComparison.Ordinal)
177:             || string.Equals(Path.GetFullPath(iso), resolvedInput, StringComparison.Ordinal)
178:             || string.Equals(Path.GetFullPath(section), resolvedInput, StringComparison.Ordinal)
179:             || string.Equals(Path.GetFullPath(report), resolvedInput, StringComparison.Ordinal))
180:             throw new PathValidationException("artifact_collides_with_input", "derived artifact name collides with the input file.");
181: 
182:         return (
183:             new InputValidation(stlPath, resolvedInput, stem, size, ""),
184:             new OutputValidation(resolvedOut, step, iso, section, report, SectionAvailable: true));
185:     }
186: 
187:     private static string ComputeSha256(string path)
188:     {
189:         using var sha = System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256.Create();
190:         using var fs = File.OpenRead(path);
191:         var hash = sha.ComputeHash(fs);
192:         return Convert.ToHexStringLower(hash);
193:     }
194: }

## Design: upload endpoint

### Config

```
Default empty → Program computes `StateRoot/uploads`. Need a way to expose final value; set it in Program at startup: if empty, set to Path.Combine(StateRoot, "uploads"). Then append to AllowedInputRoots so submits using uploaded files pass root validation. But careful: AllowedInputRoots is used by PathPolicy readiness checks (must exist/writable?). Readiness checks input roots exist as directories. Upload dir created at startup.

### Uploads store/helper
Create a small `Uploads` service (or put logic in an `UploadStore` class) that:
- sanitizes filename: take `Path.GetFileName(name)`, validate `.stl` extension (case-insensitive), reject empty.
- makes a target dir: `{UploadRoot}/{yyyymmdd}` or flat with unique id. Simplest collision-free: `<stem>_<uploadId><ext>`? But then subsequent output dir derive from stem. Better to keep the real filename but ensure uniqueness with an id suffix. On submit, stem used for derived artifact names; the client picked name. Collisions in upload dir rather unlikely; but if a file with same name exists, we could overwrite (upload semantics = replace) or 409. Since upload root is our staging area, overwriting on re-upload is acceptable, but better to keep unique: `{stem}_{shortid}.stl`. But then two uploads of "box.stl" → box_ab12.stl, box_cd34.stl etc. Fine and avoids clobbering derived artifact names in the same dir (since both outputs would collide with 409 anyway if same output dir). Keep unique id suffix: `{name without ext}_{id:8:x}{ext}`? Hmm, but then when output_dir omitted, outputs go next to upload → derived names come from stem which is now `box_ab12` → `box_ab12.stp`. Client sees uploaded stl_path and knows. That's fine, but nicer to give exact original name when available. 

Digression: simpler + predictable approach — upload always into uploadRoot with exact sanitized filename and return 409 if it already exists (mirrors output guard, no silent overwrite). Client controls uniqueness with filename or output_dir. Actually mirror existing semantics: no overwrite by default. I'll do: if target file exists → 409 `upload_already_exists`. Hmm but that complicates client re-runs (they'd re-upload same name → conflict). Provide query param to overwrite? Keep minimal: use unique name always via short id — simplest for the client (never conflicts) and matches "unique names avoid collisions". Client gets stl_path back to submit. I'll go unique naming.

Decision: stored as `{UploadRoot}/{yyyyMMdd}/{sanitized_stem}_{id}.stl`? The date subdir keeps the dir tidy. But PathUtils.ResolvePath and output default (input parent) works regardless. Keep it simple: flat `{UploadRoot}/{sanitizedname}` with uniqueness: if exists, append `-{id}` before ext? Let me just do: name = sanitized original; full=Combine(UploadRoot, name). If exists → insert `_{6hex}` before extension. Loop up to e.g. 5 attempts then append full random. Fine.

- stream copy with SHA256 incremental + size cap: use `FileStream` direct to temp file in same dir (atomic rename), copy from body in chunks, count bytes; abort if > MaxInputSizeBytes → throw `InputTooLargeException` mapped to 413 or 400 input_too_large. Return 400 `input_too_large` consistent with submit. Actually HTTP-wise 413 is more correct for a request body that's too big; but consistency with submit's `input_too_large` 400 helps clients. I'll use 413 with code input_too_large? The CLIENT_PROMPT table says 400 input_too_large for submit. For upload I'll use 413 RequestEntityTooLarge with `{code:"input_too_large"}` — document it. Hmm, easier for client to treat uniformly = 400. But 400 for "body too big" is unusual; ASP.NET often does 413. I'll do 413 and document.

296:         using var r = await ReadAsync(readyz);
297:         Assert.True(r.RootElement.GetProperty("ready").GetBoolean() || !r.RootElement.GetProperty("ready").GetBoolean());
298:         var checks = r.RootElement.GetProperty("checks");
299:         foreach (var name in new[] { "database", "state_root", "xvfb", "worker_script", "freecad", "input_roots", "output_policy" })
300:             Assert.True(checks.TryGetProperty(name, out _), $"readyz missing check: {name}");
301:     }
302: 
303:     private static async Task<JobView> PollJobAsync(HttpClient client, string jobId, int seconds)
304:     {
305:         var deadline = DateTime.UtcNow.AddSeconds(seconds);
306:         var lastStatus = "";
307:         while (DateTime.UtcNow < deadline)
308:         {
309:             var resp = await client.GetAsync($"/v1/jobs/{jobId}");
310:             var doc = await JsonDocument.ParseAsync(await resp.Content.ReadAsStreamAsync());
311:             lastStatus = doc.RootElement.GetProperty("status").GetString()!;
312:             var errorCode = doc.RootElement.TryGetProperty("error_code", out var e) && e.ValueKind == JsonValueKind.String ? e.GetString() : null;
313:             if (lastStatus is "succeeded" or "skipped" or "failed" or "cancelled")
314:             {
315:                 return new JobView(true, lastStatus, errorCode);
316:             }
317:             doc.Dispose();
318:             await Task.Delay(200);
319:         }
320:         throw new Xunit.Sdk.XunitException($"job did not reach terminal state within {seconds}s (status={lastStatus})");
321:     }
322: 
323:     private sealed record JobView(bool Terminal, string Status, string? ErrorCode);
324: }

### UploadStore (new file src/fcadserve/Api/UploadStore.cs)
Actually put it under a new folder? It's an application service with storage. Put in `Api/` is pragmatic (endpoints use it). I'll create `src/fcadserve/Api/UploadStore.cs`.

```csharp
using System.Security.Cryptography;
using FcadServe.Options;

namespace FcadServe.Api;

public sealed class UploadFilenameException(string code, string message) : Exception(message)
{
    public string Code { get; } = code;
}

public sealed class UploadTooLargeException(long limit) : Exception($"uploaded file exceeds the maximum of {limit} bytes.")
{
    public long Limit { get; } = limit;
}

/// <summary>Accepts raw STL uploads into a staging directory and returns the
/// on-disk path to submit. Stored files are unique; uploads never overwrite.</summary>
public sealed class UploadStore(ServiceOptions options, ILogger<UploadStore> logger)
{
    private readonly string _root = options.UploadRoot; // already resolved in Program
        
    public static string ResolveName(string? requested) 
    {
        // returns sanitized "stem.ext" or throws
    }

    public async Task<StoreUpload(...)>   // actually define method
}
```

Let me define record `UploadResult(string Path, string Name, long SizeBytes, string Sha256)`.

Method:
```csharp
public async Task<UploadResult> StoreAsync(string? requestedName, Stream body, CancellationToken ct)
```
- name = SanitizeName(requestedName):
   - if empty → throw missing_filename
   - filename = Path.GetFileName(requested.Replace('\\','/')) — handle both separators. Path.GetFileName on linux won't strip backslashes, so manually strip: take substring after last '/' and after last '\\'. Actually Path.GetFileName treats '\\' as valid char on unix. So sanitize: requested = requested.Replace('\\', '/'); then GetFileName.
   - if no extension or not .stl → throw unsupported_extension
- Ensure dir exists: Directory.CreateDirectory(_root).
- unique full path: candidate = Combine(_root, name); if exists, insert $"_{Random.Shared.Next...}{:x8}" before extension (loop).
- write to temp file in _root (Combine(_root, $".{n}.tmp-{guid}")):
   - using FileStream, buffer copy loop; count bytes; if > MaxInputSizeBytes → throw UploadTooLargeException. On exception, delete temp (finally).
   - hash incrementally with SHA256.
- File.Move(tmp, final).
- return result with computed sha.

Sanitize also reject name that equals "." or ".." etc -> GetFileName handles. Also reject control/empty ext.

### Endpoint
In Endpoints.MapEndpoints:
```csharp
v1.MapPost("/uploads", async (HttpRequest req, UploadStore uploads, CancellationToken ct) =>
{
    string? name = req.Query["name"];
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(name))
        name = ParseContentDispositionFilename(req);
    try
    {
        var result = await uploads.StoreAsync(name, req.Body, ct);
        return Results.Json(new
        {
            upload_id = result.Id,
            name = result.Name,
            stl_path = result.Path,
            size_bytes = result.SizeBytes,
            sha256 = result.Sha256,
            submit_url = "/v1/jobs",
        }, statusCode: StatusCodes.Status201Created);
    }
    catch (UploadFilenameException ex) => 400 Error(ex.Code, ex.Message)
    catch (UploadTooLargeException) => 413 (code input_too_large)
});
```
Need upload_id: return GUID-ish. Use `"u_" + 8 hex` generated in store.

ParseContentDispositionFilename: read `req.Headers.ContentDisposition.ToString()`, find `filename=` (may be quoted). Simplify: split on ';', find token starting with "filename=", trim quotes. Good enough; document primary `?name=`.

Note: HttpRequest binds body streaming; must limit? We do our own counting. Kestrel default max request body size is ~30MB (28.6MB); MaxInputSizeBytes default 50MB > default body limit → for >28.6MB uploads Kestrel would throw. Enable unlimited: in Program set `builder.WebHost.ConfigureKestrel(o => o.Limits.MaxRequestBodySize = null)` and enforce our own limit. Since we already enforce max input size (default 50MB), setting null is safe. But in tests with tiny max, they set env; fine.

Actually careful: Apply on the endpoint only would need IEndpointRequestSizeLimitMetadata. Simpler: set Kestrel MaxRequestBodySize=null globally and rely on UploadStore size enforcement for uploads; submits (small json) unaffected. Do that in Program.

## Assistant (Build · Big Pickle · 12.9s)

Now the `UploadStore`:

**Tool: write**

**Input:**
```json
{
  "content": "using System.Security.Cryptography;\nusing FcadServe.Options;\nusing Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;\n\nnamespace FcadServe.Api;\n\n/// <summary>Thrown when an upload is missing a usable filename or the name is not an .stl.</summary>\npublic sealed class UploadFilenameException(string code, string message) : Exception(message)\n{\n    public string Code { get; } = code;\n}\n\n/// <summary>Thrown when a streamed upload exceeds the configured input size limit.</summary>\npublic sealed class UploadTooLargeException(long limit)\n    : Exception($\"uploaded file exceeds the maximum of {limit} bytes.\")\n{\n    public long Limit { get; } = limit;\n}\n\npublic sealed record UploadResult(string Id, string Name, string Path, long SizeBytes, string Sha256);\n\n/// <summary>\n/// Accepts raw STL uploads into a staging directory and returns the absolute\n/// on-disk path a client can submit. Stored names are unique (uploads never\n/// overwrite), and files are streamed with a running SHA-256 so the size limit\n/// and checksum are computed without buffering the whole body in memory.\n/// </summary>\npublic sealed class UploadStore(ServiceOptions options, ILogger<UploadStore> logger)\n{\n    private static readonly string[] AllowedExtensions = [\".stl\"];\n\n    private readonly string _root = options.UploadRoot;\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Streams the request body into the upload root under a unique name derived\n    /// from the sanitized requested filename. Throws <see cref=\"UploadFilenameException\"/>\n    /// or <see cref=\"UploadTooLargeException\"/> on invalid/max-size input.\n    /// </summary>\n    public async Task<UploadResult> StoreAsync(string? requestedName, Stream body, CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        var name = SanitizeName(requestedName);\n        var target = UniquePath(name);\n\n        Directory.CreateDirectory(_root);\n        var tmp = Path.Combine(_root, $\".{name}.{Guid.NewGuid():N}.tmp\");\n        try\n        {\n            long size = 0;\n            byte[] digest;\n            using (var sha = SHA256.Create())\n            using (var fs = new FileStream(tmp, FileMode.CreateNew, FileAccess.Write, FileShare.None, 64 * 1024, useAsync: true))\n            {\n                var buffer = new byte[64 * 1024];\n                int read;\n                while ((read = await body.ReadAsync(buffer, ct)) > 0)\n                {\n                    size += read;\n                    if (size > options.Jobs.MaxInputSizeBytes)\n                        throw new UploadTooLargeException(options.Jobs.MaxInputSizeBytes);\n                    await fs.WriteAsync(buffer.AsMemory(0, read), ct);\n                    sha.TransformBlock(buffer, 0, read, null, 0);\n                }\n                sha.TransformFinalBlock(buffer, 0, 0);\n                digest = sha.Hash!;\n            }\n\n            if (size == 0)\n                throw new UploadFilenameException(\"empty_upload\", \"uploaded file is empty.\");\n\n            File.Move(tmp, target);\n            logger.LogInformation(\"Upload {Id}: stored {Bytes} bytes as {Path}\", name, size, target);\n\n            var id = $\"u_{Guid.NewGuid():N}\"[..11];\n            return new UploadResult(id, Path.GetFileName(target), target, size, Convert.ToHexStringLower(digest));\n        }\n        finally\n        {\n            try { if (File.Exists(tmp)) File.Delete(tmp); } catch { /* best effort */ }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>Reduces any client-supplied name to a bare \"stem.ext\", validates the extension.</summary>\n    public static string SanitizeName(string? requested)\n    {\n        var name = (requested ?? \"\").Replace('\\\\', '/');\n        name = name[(name.LastIndexOf('/') + 1)..];\n        name = name.Trim().Trim('\"').Trim('.', ' ');\n        if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(name))\n            throw new UploadFilenameException(\"missing_filename\", \"a filename is required (use ?name=part.stl or a Content-Disposition header).\");\n\n        var ext = Path.GetExtension(name);\n        if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(ext) || !AllowedExtensions.Contains(ext, StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase))\n            throw new UploadFilenameException(\"unsupported_extension\",\n                $\"unsupported extension '{ext}' (allowed: .stl).\");\n        return name;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>Picks a target in the upload root that does not exist yet.</summary>\n    private string UniquePath(string name)\n    {\n        var stem = Path.GetFileNameWithoutExtension(name);\n        var ext = Path.GetExtension(name);\n        var candidate = Path.Combine(_root, name);\n        for (var i = 0; i < 8 && File.Exists(candidate); i++)\n            candidate = Path.Combine(_root, $\"{stem}_{Random.Shared.NextInt64():x8}{ext}\");\n        for (var i = 0; File.Exists(candidate); i++)\n            candidate = Path.Combine(_root, $\"{stem}_{Random.Shared.NextInt64():x8}_{i:x}{ext}\");\n        return Path.GetFullPath(candidate);\n    }\n}",
  "filePath": "/home/fat64/github/FreeCADService/src/fcadserve/Api/UploadStore.cs"
}

## Assistant (Build · Big Pickle · 11.5s)

_Thinking:_

Wait — I have an issue with the Theory test. The `InlineData("/v1/uploads?name=../../evil.stl", "unsupported_extension")` case URL contains `..%2F` in my check but I wrote `..%2F` — the InlineData contains `../..` literal which will be URL-decoded by HttpClient? Actually the query string in the URL is passed as-is to the request; Uri may normalize `../`? `http://.../v1/uploads?name=../../evil.stl` — the query part `name=../../evil.stl` isn't subject to path normalization (it's after `?`), so it's sent literally. ASP.NET decodes it to `../../evil.stl`. SanitizeName turns into `evil.stl` (after last '/') → valid .stl → 201, not 400. My theory expects "unsupported_extension" for that case which would FAIL.

I explicitly skip that case in the theory (the weird `if (expectedCode == "unsupported_extension" && url.Contains("..%2F"))` check — but the URL contains `../..` not `..%2F`, so the skip never triggers and the assertion `400 unsupported_extension` would fail).

This theory approach is messy. Let me fix: remove the traversal InlineData from the theory and from the skip logic entirely, since traversal is covered by its own dedicated Fact (`Upload_path_traversal_name_is_sanitized_and_stored_inside_root`).

## Assistant (Build · Big Pickle · 10.0s)

_Thinking:_

The traversal fact uses `..%2F..%2Fevil.stl` in the URL — HttpClient will send that raw in the query. Uri parsing: does `HttpClient` percent-encode `%` again? No — a `%2F` in the URL stays `%2F` (already encoded), and ASP.NET decodes to `../`, then SanitizeName strips to `evil.stl`. Should work. The only concern: `new Uri("...?name=..%2F..%2Fevil.stl")` is valid, doesn't change. Good.

But wait, my SanitizeName: name = `../evil.stl` after decode... actually decode of `%2F` gives `/`, so `../../evil.stl`. `Replace('\\','/')` no-op; `LastIndexOf('/')` → strip to `evil.stl`. Good → 201.

One more: the traversal fact asserts `stlPath.StartsWith(box.Uploads)`. Uploads = State/uploads. The default uploadroot derives from state root env; `Path.GetFullPath` applied. Good.

Also in the happy-path test I assert `Assert.StartsWith(box.Uploads, stlPath)`. Since Path.GetFullPath on a temp path => /tmp/fcadserve-xxx/state/uploads/...; box.Uploads = Path.Combine(State,"uploads") = same absolute (temp dir is absolute). StartsWith works.

Now the SampleStl string: PadRight(80) then binary count bytes... The string is a C# literal with `\x00` escapes. When using Encoding.UTF8.GetBytes, the `\x00` chars encode to a single 0x00 byte in UTF8. `\x01` → 0x01. The facet count = 4 bytes little-endian `\x01\x00\x00\x00` → 1. Fine. Not that it matters; upload doesn't parse STL, it just stores bytes and computes sha/size. The content just needs to be non-empty. Good.

Now build + run tests.
